<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For every fixed treewidth bound, the shortest-path metrics of finite connected graphs with arbitrary positive real edge lengths embed into real <i>L</i><sub>1</sub> with uniformly bounded distortion. This resolves the bounded-treewidth case of the Gupta–Newman–Rabinovich–Sinclair conjecture positively.

- **claim-main-embedding** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every integer $k\ge2$ there is a finite constant $C_k\ge1$ with the following property. If a finite connected graph $G=(V,E)$ admits a tree decomposition with bags of size at most $k$, then, for every edge-length function $\ell:E\to(0,\infty)$, there is a map $F$ from $V$ into a real $L_1$ space such that $$\label{eq:main-embedding} d_{G,\ell}(u,v) \le \\|F(u)-F(v)\\|_1 \le C_k\,d_{G,\ell}(u,v) \qquad(u,v\in V).$$ The constant is independent of the number of vertices, the depth of the decomposition, and all ratios of edge lengths.
  - Explanation: Shortest-path metrics of connected graphs with a bounded bag size embed into real L1 with distortion depending only on the bag bound. The constant does not depend on graph size, decomposition depth or edge-length ratios.
- **claim-separator-properties** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-separator)): The following statements hold for a tree decomposition of a connected graph.* 1. *If a bag $B$ lies on the tree path between bags $A$ and $C$, every graph path from a vertex of $A$ to a vertex of $C$ meets $B$.* 2. *For adjacent bags $A,C$, their intersection separates the unions of bags on the two sides of the corresponding tree edge. In particular, adjacent nonempty bags have nonempty intersection.* 3. *If $A_0,\ldots,A_r$ occur in this order on a tree path and $x\in A_r$, then $d(x,A_i)$ is nonincreasing in $i$. Moreover, if $B$ lies between $A$ and a bag containing $x$, some $v\in B$ on a shortest path from $A$ to $x$ satisfies $$\label{eq:separator-geodesic} d(x,A)-d(v,A)=d(x,v).$$
  - Explanation: Bags on a tree path separate graph paths, adjacent bags separate the two sides of the tree edge, and distances from a fixed vertex to successive bags on a path never increase.
- **claim-markov-gluing** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-gluing)): Every consistent family of bag measures has a canonical Markov gluing $\rho_\mu$ on $\Omega_V$, of mass $m$, with trace $\mu_t$ on each bag. It is independent of the chosen root and is symmetric when every bag measure is symmetric. Along a path of bags, the successive intersection assignments, with a vertex label from the corresponding endpoint bag at either end, form a Markov chain under $\rho_\mu/m$. The associated pseudometric $$D_\mu(u,v)=\rho_\mu\{\omega:\omega(u)\ne\omega(v)\}$$ is realized in a finite-dimensional real $L_1$ space.
  - Explanation: Consistent bag measures combine into one global cut measure by sampling each bag conditionally on its overlap with its parent. This gives exact bag marginals, a Markov chain along bag paths and an L1 realization of the glued pseudometric.
- **claim-symmetric-markov-flow** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-markov-flow)): For every integer $w\geq2$ there is a finite constant $C(w)\geq1$ with the following property. Suppose a symmetric Markov flow graph has at most $w$ states per layer, first layer $\{s_0,s_1\}$, and last layer $\{t_0,t_1\}$, and its involutions interchange the two states in each endpoint layer. Every capacity-respecting flow from $s_0$ to $t_1$ has value at most $$C(w)\Pr[X_0=s_0,\ X_r=t_1].$$ The constant is independent of the number of layers and of all transition probabilities.
  - Explanation: In a symmetric Markov flow graph with boundedly many states per layer, any feasible flow between the two endpoint states is bounded by a constant multiple of the endpoint probability, independently of the number of layers.
- **claim-uncertainty-transport** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-uncertainty)): Let $\mu$ be a consistent family of symmetric bag measures, with bags of size at most $k$. Consider the path $B_0,\ldots,B_r$ from a bag containing $y$ to a bag containing $z$, and let $P=B_j$ lie on this path. Suppose a labeled cut measure $\nu$ on $V$ satisfies $$\nu\|_{B_i}\leq\mu_{B_i}\qquad(j\leq i\leq r).$$ Then the Markov-glued pseudometric satisfies $$\label{eq:uncertainty-transport} D_\mu(y,z)\geq b_k E_\nu(z\mid P).$$ The measure $\nu$ need not be symmetric.
  - Explanation: If a cut measure stays below the bag laws along the second part of a bag path, its uncertainty at the terminal vertex forces separation of the endpoints in the glued metric, with a loss depending only on the bag bound.
- **claim-height-gap-test** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-height-gap)): Let $P\subseteq V$, $z\in V$, and $t>0$. Suppose that $F(z)$ has distance at least $t$ from both endpoints of $[-M,M]$ and that $$\|F(z)-F(s)\|\geq t\qquad(s\in P).$$ For $\nu=(\sigma_F^M)^{\mathrm{sym}}$, one has $$E_\nu(z\mid P)\geq t.$$
  - Explanation: A height gap between the target vertex and every vertex of the testing set, away from the sweep cutoffs, yields uncertainty at least that gap for the symmetrized sweep.
- **claim-launch-gap** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-launch-gap)): Let $A\subseteq V$ and let $f,g:V\to\mathbb R$ satisfy $g\|_A=0$ and $f+g,f-g\in[-M,M]^V$. Set $$\nu=(\sigma_{f+g}^M)^{\mathrm{sym}} +(\sigma_{f-g}^M)^{\mathrm{sym}}.$$ Then, for every $z\in V$, $$\label{eq:launch-uncertainty} E_\nu(z\mid A)\geq2\|g(z)\|.$$ Moreover, for every $u,v\in V$, $$\label{eq:paired-separation} \begin{split} D_\nu(u,v) &=\|f(u)-f(v)+g(u)-g(v)\|\\ &\quad+\|f(u)-f(v)-g(u)+g(v)\| \geq2\|g(u)-g(v)\|. \end{split}$$
  - Explanation: Two opposite perturbations that vanish on a launch bag produce uncertainty at any vertex at least twice the perturbation size there, and their separation of any pair is at least twice the perturbation difference.
- **claim-affine-trace-update** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-trace-update)): Let $P\subseteq V$, and suppose that particles $F_1,F_2$ admit a common weak increasing order on $P$. Put $f=(F_1+F_2)/2$, and let $g:V\to\mathbb R$ vanish on $P$. If the four functions $F_1,F_2,f+g,f-g$ take values in $[-M,M]$, then $$\left.(\sigma_{F_1}^M+\sigma_{F_2}^M)\right\|_P =\left.(\sigma_{f+g}^M+\sigma_{f-g}^M)\right\|_P.$$ The equality remains true after symmetrization. For nonempty $P$, it includes the masses of both constant assignments on $P$.
  - Explanation: Replacing two particles that share a weak order on a set by the average plus and minus a perturbation vanishing there leaves their combined cut trace on that set unchanged, constant assignments included.
- **claim-uniform-particle-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-particle-bounds)): After any finite sequence of updates starting from zero particles, $$\operatorname{Lip}(F_i)\le L(N-1), \qquad \|F_i(v)\|\le B(N-1)\Delta \quad (1\le i\le N,\ v\in V).$$ These bounds hold for arbitrary choices of the updated pairs and perturbations subject to the displayed bounds, regardless of the number of updates.
  - Explanation: Lipschitz constants and heights of all particles stay uniformly bounded after any number of updates, because a quadratic invariant on sums of coordinates is preserved.
- **claim-particle-realization** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-particle-realization)): Suppose a finite schedule requires at most $q$ simultaneous reserved pairs in each memory copy, including any temporary reservation made before another is released. Updates may be made at the current bag, or on a child-specific copy at its parent bag before moving to the child. Before processing the root, updates at the empty interface are also allowed. Every update uses a perturbation satisfying the bounds above and vanishing on its interface.* *Choose $$N=2q+k!+2, \qquad M=\bigl(B(N-1)+2\bigr)\Delta.$$ Then all requested updates and reservations can be carried out. They produce symmetric bag measures $\mu_t$ with the following properties.* 1. *Each $\mu_t$ has the same total mass $m=2NM$, and neighboring bag measures have equal traces on their intersection.* 2. *Every particle height is at distance at least $2\Delta$ from each endpoint of the sweep interval $[-M,M]$.* 3. *The pseudometric $D_\mu$ obtained by Markov gluing satisfies $$D_\mu(u,v)\le U\,d(u,v) \quad\text{for all }u,v\in V, \qquad U=NL(N-1).$$* 4. *If a pair is reserved from a launch bag through a later bag on a descending path, its fixed symmetric two-particle cut measure has trace dominated by $\mu_t$ at every bag on that segment. The segment includes the launch and release bags, even when the particles are reused at the release bag. A reservation launched at the empty interface has the same property from the root onward.* *The statements hold for each realization of any auxiliary randomness. For measurable randomized choices, taking expectations also gives an $L_1$ pseudometric with the same upper bound.
  - Explanation: A finite pool of particles, sized by the maximum number of simultaneous reservations and the number of weak orders on a bag, realizes any admissible schedule of updates and reservations with consistent symmetric bag measures and a uniformly bounded glued pseudometric.
- **claim-padded-partitions** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-padded)): There are constants $\alpha\in(0,1/8]$ and $\pi\in(0,1]$ such that, for every metric as above and every $r>0$, there is a random partition $\mathcal P$ of $V$ satisfying $$\operatorname{diam}(C)\leq r\quad(C\in\mathcal P), \qquad \mathbb P\bigl[B_d(x,\alpha r)\subseteq\mathcal P(x)\bigr]\geq\pi \quad(x\in V).$$ Here $\mathcal P(x)$ denotes the part containing $x$.
  - Explanation: Bounded-treewidth metrics admit random partitions at every scale with bounded part diameter in which a small ball around each vertex stays inside one part with probability bounded below; the constants depend only on the bag bound.
- **claim-disjoint-bumps** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-disjoint-bumps)): Let $E_1,\ldots,E_m$ be pairwise disjoint subsets of a metric space. For each $i$, let $\psi_i$ be a nonnegative $1$-Lipschitz function vanishing outside $E_i$, and let $u_i\in[0,1]$. Then $\sum_i u_i\psi_i$ is $1$-Lipschitz.
  - Explanation: A combination with coefficients in the unit interval of nonnegative unit-Lipschitz bumps supported on pairwise disjoint sets is again unit-Lipschitz.
- **claim-cobag-embedding** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-cobag)): For every rooted tree decomposition with nonempty bags of size at most $k$, there is an $L_1$ pseudometric $D_{\mathrm{bag}}$ on $V$ and constants $U_{\mathrm{bag}}<\infty$ and $a_{\mathrm{bag}}>0$, depending only on $k$, such that $$D_{\mathrm{bag}}(u,v)\leq U_{\mathrm{bag}}d(u,v) \quad(u,v\in V),$$ and $$D_{\mathrm{bag}}(u,v)\geq a_{\mathrm{bag}}d(u,v) \quad\text{whenever $u,v$ belong to a common bag}.$$ One may take $$R=4\binom{k}{2},\qquad N=2R+k!+2,\qquad U_{\mathrm{bag}}=3N(N-1),\qquad a_{\mathrm{bag}}=\frac{\pi\alpha}{64}.$$
  - Explanation: Reserving one particle pair per active dyadic scale along a rooted decomposition gives an L1 pseudometric with a uniform upper bound and a uniform lower bound for pairs sharing a bag.
- **claim-ordinary-launch-noise** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-ordinary-noise)): Let $A\subseteq V$ be nonempty, put $h(z)=d(z,A)$, and let $0<\eta<1/4$. One can sample a random function $$g=\xi h+v,$$ using randomness independent of any previously sampled data. The function $v$ is constructed by first sampling a finite indexed family of nonnegative functions $(\varphi_i)_{i\in\mathcal I}$ on $V$, called bumps, and then setting $$v=\sum_{i\in\mathcal I}U_i\varphi_i.$$ Conditional on the bump family, the coefficients $U_i$ are independent and uniform on $[0,1]$. The following properties hold:* 1. *$\xi$ is uniform on $[1,2]$ and independent of the bump family and all its coefficients; $v$ is $1$-Lipschitz and satisfies $0\leq v\leq\eta h/2$. Consequently $g\|_A=0$, $\operatorname{Lip}(g)\leq3$, and $$h\leq g\leq \tfrac52h\leq\tfrac52\Delta.$$* 2. *Set $c=\alpha/8$ and $\rho=\pi/2$. For each fixed $x$ with $D_0=h(x)>0$, there is an event of probability at least $\rho$, determined by the bump family, on which an index $i(x)\in\mathcal I$, also determined by that family, selects a bump $\varphi_x=\varphi_{i(x)}$ satisfying $$\varphi_x(x)\geq c\eta D_0, \qquad \varphi_x(z)=0\quad\text{if }d(x,z)>4\eta D_0.$$ Write $U_x=U_{i(x)}$. Conditional on the bump family, $U_x$ is uniform and independent of $\xi$, of the other bump coefficients, and of all previously sampled data. In particular, $v=v^{\setminus x}+U_x\varphi_x$, where the remaining coefficients defining $v^{\setminus x}$ are independent of $U_x$.* *Different vertices may select the same bump and coefficient. The constants in these assertions are independent of $\eta$, $A$, the number of vertices, and the ratio of the edge lengths. Only finitely many random partitions and coefficients are required.
  - Explanation: A launch perturbation combines a random multiple of the distance to the launch bag with small independent local bumps. It stays Lipschitz, vanishes on the launch bag and gives each vertex a bump coefficient that is uniform and independent of the rest.
- **claim-cache-well-defined** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cache-defined)): The displayed distance list is nonincreasing, and an eligible eviction always exists. The cache has at most $n_0$ entries after an eviction and at most $n_0+1$ simultaneous reservations when the proposed and evicted anchors are both included.
  - Explanation: Distances from a bag vertex to anchors are nonincreasing in age, protected entries are few, so an eviction candidate always exists and the number of simultaneous reservations is bounded.
- **claim-anchor-tests** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-anchor-tests)): Let a path of bags run from a bag containing $y$ to a later bag containing $z$.* 1. *If an anchor $A$ lies on this path and its reservation lasts from $A$ through the later bag, then $$\label{eq:anchor-launch-test} D_{\mathrm{anc}}(y,z)\geq b_k d(z,A).$$* 2. *Suppose a testing bag $P$ lies on the path and a reserved particle $F$ is unchanged from $P$ through the later bag. If, with probability at least $\theta$, it satisfies $$\|F(z)-F(s)\|\geq t\quad\text{for every }s\in P,$$ where $0<t\leq\operatorname{diam}(V)$, then $$\label{eq:anchor-height-test} D_{\mathrm{anc}}(y,z)\geq b_k\theta t.$$ In the second assertion the tested reservation and vertices are chosen without consulting launch randomness.* *Both assertions allow the later bag to be the eviction bag.
  - Explanation: A surviving reservation along a bag path forces separation of the endpoints, either through the distance to its launch bag or through a probable height gap at a testing bag.
- **claim-uniform-lower-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-combined-lower)): For each fixed $k\geq2$ there is a constant $\lambda_k>0$ such that, for every admissible weighted graph with a tree decomposition of bag size at most $k$ and every pair of vertices $u,v$, $$\label{eq:combined-lower} D_{\mathrm{bag}}(u,v)+D_{\mathrm{anc}}(u,v) \geq\lambda_k d(u,v).$$
  - Explanation: The sum of the common-bag and anchor pseudometrics is bounded below by a positive constant times the graph distance for every pair, with the constant depending only on the bag bound.
- **claim-finite-limiting-rules** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-limiting-rules)): 1. *Of finitely many alternatives whose union holds eventually, at least one holds eventually. Finitely many eventual assertions hold simultaneously eventually.* 2. *A sequence in $[0,1]$ that is not infinitesimal is eventually bounded below by a positive real constant. A finite minimum of such sequences has the same property.* 3. *Sequences of times in finite linearly ordered paths are linearly ordered modulo $\mathcal U$. If $\delta_t\in[0,1]$ is nonincreasing along each path, a time sequence with noninfinitesimal $\delta_t$ precedes every time sequence with infinitesimal $\delta_t$.
  - Explanation: Elementary ultrafilter facts: finite unions and intersections of eventual statements behave as expected, non-infinitesimal sequences are bounded below, and times along finite paths are linearly ordered.
- **claim-youngest-crossing-anchor** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-youngest-crossing)): There are at most $n_0+1$ distinct crossing anchor sequences. The set is nonempty and has a youngest member $A_*$.
  - Explanation: Under the normalized failure, only boundedly many anchors start early and survive late. The set contains the root, so it has a youngest member.
- **claim-old-distant-anchor** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-old-distant-anchor)): The launch of $A_*$ is strictly above $S$, and $D_0>H$ eventually. In particular, $A_*$ is present at every selected early bag from $S$ onward.
  - Explanation: The youngest crossing anchor was launched above the least common ancestor bag and lies far from the chosen endpoint, since otherwise the noise tests would already separate the pair.
- **claim-anchor-obstruction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-anchor-obstruction)): Let $B$ be any selected early sequence of bags from $S$ to $X$ and let $p\in B$ be nearest to $x$. Put $\delta=d(x,B)$. There is a sequence $q\in B$ such that, eventually, $$\label{eq:anchor-obstruction} \frac{\delta}{4}\leq d(p,q)\leq10D_0.$$
  - Explanation: At every early bag some vertex lies at an intermediate distance from the vertex nearest the endpoint. Otherwise independent noise sources would produce a height gap contradicting the failure.
- **claim-protected-successor** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-protected-successor)): At any selected early eviction test $B$, suppose the immediate successor $A'$ of $A_*$ in the inclusive cache list was launched at or after $S$. Then $A'$ is protected and cannot be evicted at this test.
  - Explanation: The distance obstruction places one eviction threshold between the distances of the old anchor and its immediate successor, so that successor is protected from eviction at early tests.
- **claim-younger-crossing-anchor** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-younger-crossing)): Under the normalized failure assumption <a href="#eq:normalized-failure" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:normalized-failure">[eq:normalized-failure]</a>, there is a crossing anchor younger than $A_*$.
  - Explanation: Protection of the immediate successor makes it survive to a late bag, giving a crossing anchor younger than the youngest one and thus a contradiction.

If the four functions $F_1,F_2,f+g,f-g$ take values in $[-M,M]$, then $$\\left.(\\sigma_{F_1}^M+\\sigma_{F_2}^M)\\right|_P\n =\\left.(\\sigma_{f+g}^M+\\sigma_{f-g}^M)\\right|_P.$$ The equality remains true after symmetrization.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-particle-bounds",

          "source_id": "PM-089.2"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-particle-realization",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose a finite schedule requires at most $q$ simultaneous reserved pairs in each memory copy, including any temporary reservation made before another is released. Updates may be made at the current bag, or on a child-specific copy at its parent bag before moving to the child. Before processing the root, updates at the empty interface are also allowed. Every update uses a perturbation satisfying the bounds above and vanishing on its interface.*\n\n*Choose $$N=2q+k!+2,\n \\qquad\n M=\\bigl(B(N-1)+2\\bigr)\\Delta.$$ Then all requested updates and reservations can be carried out. They produce symmetric bag measures $\\mu_t$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *Each $\\mu_t$ has the same total mass $m=2NM$, and neighboring bag measures have equal traces on their intersection.*\n\n2.  *Every particle height is at distance at least $2\\Delta$ from each endpoint of the sweep interval $[-M,M]$.*\n\n3.  *The pseudometric $D_\\mu$ obtained by Markov gluing satisfies $$D_\\mu(u,v)\\le U\\,d(u,v)\n     \\quad\\text{for all }u,v\\in V,\n     \\qquad U=NL(N-1).$$*\n\n4.  *If a pair is reserved from a launch bag through a later bag on a descending path, its fixed symmetric two-particle cut measure has trace dominated by $\\mu_t$ at every bag on that segment. The segment includes the launch and release bags, even when the particles are reused at the release bag. A reservation launched at the empty interface has the same property from the root onward.*\n\n*The statements hold for each realization of any auxiliary randomness. For measurable randomized choices, taking expectations also gives an $L_1$ pseudometric with the same upper bound.",
        "source_id": "PM-089.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-padded",
        "exact_quote": "There are constants $\\alpha\\in(0,1/8]$ and $\\pi\\in(0,1]$ such that, for every metric as above and every $r>0$, there is a random partition $\\mathcal P$ of $V$ satisfying $$\\operatorname{diam}(C)\\leq r\\quad(C\\in\\mathcal P),\n  \\qquad\n  \\mathbb P\\bigl[B_d(x,\\alpha r)\\subseteq\\mathcal P(x)\\bigr]\\geq\\pi\n  \\quad(x\\in V).$$ Here $\\mathcal P(x)$ denotes the part containing $x$.",
        "source_id": "PM-089.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-thm-padded",

          "source_id": "PM-089.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There are constants $\\alpha\\in(0,1/8]$ and $\\pi\\in(0,1]$ such that, for every metric as above and every $r>0$, there is a random partition $\\mathcal P$ of $V$ satisfying $$\\operatorname{diam}(C)\\leq r\\quad(C\\in\\mathcal P),\n  \\qquad\n  \\mathbb P\\bigl[B_d(x,\\alpha r)\\subseteq\\mathcal P(x)\\bigr]\\geq\\pi\n  \\quad(x\\in V).$$ Here $\\mathcal P(x)$ denotes the part containing $x$.",
      "explanation": "Bounded-treewidth metrics admit random partitions at every scale with bounded part diameter in which a small ball around each vertex stays inside one part with probability bounded below; the constants depend only on the bag bound.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-cobag",
        "exact_quote": "For every rooted tree decomposition with nonempty bags of size at most $k$, there is an $L_1$ pseudometric $D_{\\mathrm{bag}}$ on $V$ and constants $U_{\\mathrm{bag}}<\\infty$ and $a_{\\mathrm{bag}}>0$, depending only on $k$, such that $$D_{\\mathrm{bag}}(u,v)\\leq U_{\\mathrm{bag}}d(u,v)\n       \\quad(u,v\\in V),$$ and $$D_{\\mathrm{bag}}(u,v)\\geq a_{\\mathrm{bag}}d(u,v)\n       \\quad\\text{whenever $u,v$ belong to a common bag}.$$ One may take $$R=4\\binom{k}{2},\\qquad N=2R+k!+2,\\qquad\n  U_{\\mathrm{bag}}=3N(N-1),\\qquad\n  a_{\\mathrm{bag}}=\\frac{\\pi\\alpha}{64}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-089.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-cobag",
          "exact_quote": "For every rooted tree decomposition with nonempty bags of size at most $k$, there is an $L_1$ pseudometric $D_{\\mathrm{bag}}$ on $V$ and constants $U_{\\mathrm{bag}}<\\infty$ and $a_{\\mathrm{bag}}>0$, depending only on $k$, such that $$D_{\\mathrm{bag}}(u,v)\\leq U_{\\mathrm{bag}}d(u,v)\n       \\quad(u,v\\in V),$$ and $$D_{\\mathrm{bag}}(u,v)\\geq a_{\\mathrm{bag}}d(u,v)\n       \\quad\\text{whenever $u,v$ belong to a common bag}.$$ One may take $$R=4\\binom{k}{2},\\qquad N=2R+k!+2,\\qquad\n  U_{\\mathrm{bag}}=3N(N-1),\\qquad\n  a_{\\mathrm{bag}}=\\frac{\\pi\\alpha}{64}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-089.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For every rooted tree decomposition with nonempty bags of size at most $k$, there is an $L_1$ pseudometric $D_{\\mathrm{bag}}$ on $V$ and constants $U_{\\mathrm{bag}}<\\infty$ and $a_{\\mathrm{bag}}>0$, depending only on $k$, such that $$D_{\\mathrm{bag}}(u,v)\\leq U_{\\mathrm{bag}}d(u,v)\n       \\quad(u,v\\in V),$$ and $$D_{\\mathrm{bag}}(u,v)\\geq a_{\\mathrm{bag}}d(u,v)\n       \\quad\\text{whenever $u,v$ belong to a common bag}.$$ One may take $$R=4\\binom{k}{2},\\qquad N=2R+k!+2,\\qquad\n  U_{\\mathrm{bag}}=3N(N-1),\\qquad\n  a_{\\mathrm{bag}}=\\frac{\\pi\\alpha}{64}.$$",
      "explanation": "Reserving one particle pair per active dyadic scale along a rooted decomposition gives an L1 pseudometric with a uniform upper bound and a uniform lower bound for pairs sharing a bag.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-ordinary-noise",
        "exact_quote": "Let $A\\subseteq V$ be nonempty, put $h(z)=d(z,A)$, and let $0<\\eta<1/4$. One can sample a random function $$g=\\xi h+v,$$ using randomness independent of any previously sampled data. The function $v$ is constructed by first sampling a finite indexed family of nonnegative functions $(\\varphi_i)_{i\\in\\mathcal I}$ on $V$, called bumps, and then setting $$v=\\sum_{i\\in\\mathcal I}U_i\\varphi_i.$$ Conditional on the bump family, the coefficients $U_i$ are independent and uniform on $[0,1]$. The following properties hold:*\n\n1.  *$\\xi$ is uniform on $[1,2]$ and independent of the bump family and all its coefficients; $v$ is $1$-Lipschitz and satisfies $0\\leq v\\leq\\eta h/2$. Consequently $g|_A=0$, $\\operatorname{Lip}(g)\\leq3$, and $$h\\leq g\\leq \\tfrac52h\\leq\\tfrac52\\Delta.$$*\n\n2.  *Set $c=\\alpha/8$ and $\\rho=\\pi/2$. For each fixed $x$ with $D_0=h(x)>0$, there is an event of probability at least $\\rho$, determined by the bump family, on which an index $i(x)\\in\\mathcal I$, also determined by that family, selects a bump $\\varphi_x=\\varphi_{i(x)}$ satisfying $$\\varphi_x(x)\\geq c\\eta D_0,\n            \\qquad\n            \\varphi_x(z)=0\\quad\\text{if }d(x,z)>4\\eta D_0.$$ Write $U_x=U_{i(x)}$. Conditional on the bump family, $U_x$ is uniform and independent of $\\xi$, of the other bump coefficients, and of all previously sampled data. In particular, $v=v^{\\setminus x}+U_x\\varphi_x$, where the remaining coefficients defining $v^{\\setminus x}$ are independent of $U_x$.*\n\n*Different vertices may select the same bump and coefficient. The constants in these assertions are independent of $\\eta$, $A$, the number of vertices, and the ratio of the edge lengths. Only finitely many random partitions and coefficients are required.",
        "source_id": "PM-089.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-ordinary-noise",
          "exact_quote": "Let $A\\subseteq V$ be nonempty, put $h(z)=d(z,A)$, and let $0<\\eta<1/4$. The following properties hold:*\n\n1.  *$\\xi$ is uniform on $[1,2]$ and independent of the bump family and all its coefficients; $v$ is $1$-Lipschitz and satisfies $0\\leq v\\leq\\eta h/2$. Consequently $g|_A=0$, $\\operatorname{Lip}(g)\\leq3$, and $$h\\leq g\\leq \\tfrac52h\\leq\\tfrac52\\Delta.$$*\n\n2.  *Set $c=\\alpha/8$ and $\\rho=\\pi/2$. For each fixed $x$ with $D_0=h(x)>0$, there is an event of probability at least $\\rho$, determined by the bump family, on which an index $i(x)\\in\\mathcal I$, also determined by that family, selects a bump $\\varphi_x=\\varphi_{i(x)}$ satisfying $$\\varphi_x(x)\\geq c\\eta D_0,\n            \\qquad\n            \\varphi_x(z)=0\\quad\\text{if }d(x,z)>4\\eta D_0.$$ Write $U_x=U_{i(x)}$. Conditional on the bump family, $U_x$ is uniform and independent of $\\xi$, of the other bump coefficients, and of all previously sampled data. The constants in these assertions are independent of $\\eta$, $A$, the number of vertices, and the ratio of the edge lengths. Only finitely many random partitions and coefficients are required.",

Consequently $g|_A=0$, $\\operatorname{Lip}(g)\\leq3$, and $$h\\leq g\\leq \\tfrac52h\\leq\\tfrac52\\Delta.$$*\n\n2.  *Set $c=\\alpha/8$ and $\\rho=\\pi/2$.       "explanation": "A launch perturbation combines a random multiple of the distance to the launch bag with small independent local bumps. It stays Lipschitz, vanishes on the launch bag and gives each vertex a bump coefficient that is uniform and independent of the rest.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-anchor-tests",
        "exact_quote": "Let a path of bags run from a bag containing $y$ to a later bag containing $z$.*\n\n\n1.  *If an anchor $A$ lies on this path and its reservation lasts from $A$ through the later bag, then $$\\label{eq:anchor-launch-test}\n     D_{\\mathrm{anc}}(y,z)\\geq b_k d(z,A).$$*\n\n2.  *Suppose a testing bag $P$ lies on the path and a reserved particle $F$ is unchanged from $P$ through the later bag. If, with probability at least $\\theta$, it satisfies $$|F(z)-F(s)|\\geq t\\quad\\text{for every }s\\in P,$$ where $0<t\\leq\\operatorname{diam}(V)$, then $$\\label{eq:anchor-height-test}\n     D_{\\mathrm{anc}}(y,z)\\geq b_k\\theta t.$$ In the second assertion the tested reservation and vertices are chosen without consulting launch randomness.*\n\n*Both assertions allow the later bag to be the eviction bag.",
        "source_id": "PM-089.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **graph metric** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A graph metric is the shortest-path metric $d_{G,\ell}$ of a finite connected undirected graph $G=(V,E)$ with positive real edge lengths $\ell:E\to(0,\infty)$.
- **distortion at most C** ([source](#sec:introduction)): An embedding into real $L_1$ has *distortion at most $C$* if it can be rescaled so that every image distance is between the original distance and $C$ times that distance.
- **tree decomposition** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A *tree decomposition* consists of a finite tree $T$ and subsets $B_t\subseteq V$, called bags, indexed by $t\in V(T)$: the bags cover $V$, each graph edge has both endpoints in a bag, and the bags containing any fixed vertex induce a connected subtree of $T$.
- **bag cut law** ([source](#pm-h-4)): For a bag $B$, a *bag cut law* assigns nonnegative masses to the binary labelings $\{0,1\}^B$.
- **labeled cut measure** ([source](#pm-h-10)): A *labeled cut measure* on $S$ is a finite nonnegative measure on $\Omega_S$.
- **trace of a measure** ([source](#pm-h-10)): For $R\subseteq S$, its *trace* $\nu\|_R$ is the pushforward under restriction to $R$.
- **symmetric measure** ([source](#pm-h-10)): A measure is *symmetric* if $\nu=\nu^c$.
- **sweep margin** ([source](#pm-h-12)): We say that $F$ has *sweep margin* at least $a>0$ if $\|F(v)\|\leq M-a$ for every $v\in V$.
- **particle** ([source](#sec:particles)): A *particle* is a real function on all of $V$.
- **reservation** ([source](#sec:particles)): $$ The corresponding perturbation is $e=(g(u)-g(v))/(L\,d(u,v))$, of absolute value at most one. This proves the Lipschitz bound. For each fixed $v$, apply the same invariant to $a_i=F_i(v)/(B\Delta)$, with $e=g(v)/(B\Delta)$, to obtain the value bound. The argument applies separately to every branch of a copied particle history. ◻ </details> A *reservation* freezes a pair of particles: neither particle may be used in another update while that reservation is in force.
- **anchor** ([source](#sec:anchors)): An *anchor* is a bag occurrence together with the reserved pair launched there.
- **infinitesimal sequence** ([source](#pm-h-21)): A nonnegative real sequence is *infinitesimal* if, for each $\varepsilon>0$, it is eventually smaller than $\varepsilon$.
- **early and late bag sequences** ([source](#pm-h-22)): A selected sequence of bags is called *early* if its $\delta_B$ is not infinitesimal, and *late* otherwise.
- **crossing anchor sequence** ([source](#pm-h-22)): Call a sequence of anchors *crossing* if its effective start is early and its terminal bag is late.

The argument applies separately to every branch of a copied particle history. ◻ </details> A *reservation* freezes a pair of particles: neither particle may be used in another update while that reservation is in force.",
      "interpretation": "A pair of particles held unchanged so its cut measure persists along a bag segment.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "reservation",
      "scope": "Particle lists copied per child."
    },
    {
      "definition_anchor": "sec:anchors",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "sec:anchors",
          "exact_quote": "An *anchor* is a bag occurrence together with the reserved pair launched there.",
          "source_id": "PM-089.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "An *anchor* is a bag occurrence together with the reserved pair launched there.",
      "interpretation": "A launch bag together with its reserved particle pair, kept in a bounded cache.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "anchor",
      "scope": "Anchor construction along descending paths."
    },
    {

          "exact_quote": "A nonnegative real sequence is *infinitesimal* if, for each $\\varepsilon>0$, it is eventually smaller than $\\varepsilon$.",
          "source_id": "PM-089.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "A nonnegative real sequence is *infinitesimal* if, for each $\\varepsilon>0$, it is eventually smaller than $\\varepsilon$.",

| q-main-constant-lower | C_k | ≥ | $1$ | bag size bound k at least two |
| q-treewidth-bound | treewidth bound | = | $k-1$ | bag cardinality bound k |
| q-markov-flow-constant | C(w) | ≥ | $1$ | w at least two |
| q-transport-constant | b_k | = | $C(2^k)^{-1}$ | fixed k |
| q-particle-count | N | = | $2q+k!+2$ | q simultaneous reserved pairs, bag bound k |
| q-sweep-cutoff | M | = | $\bigl(B(N-1)+2\bigr)\Delta$ | diameter Delta, perturbation height bound B |
| q-glued-upper-factor | U | = | $NL(N-1)$ | Lipschitz bound L, particle count N |
| q-particle-lipschitz | Lip(F_i) | ≤ | $L(N-1)$ | any number of updates |
| q-particle-height | \|F_i(v)\| | ≤ | $B(N-1)\Delta$ | any number of updates |
| q-padding-scale | alpha | ≤ | $1/8$ | depends only on the bag bound |
| q-padding-probability | pi | ≤ | $1$ | depends only on the bag bound |
| q-cobag-lower-factor | a_bag | = | $\frac{\pi\alpha}{64}$ | pairs sharing a bag |
| q-cobag-upper-factor | U_bag | = | $3N(N-1)$ | all pairs of vertices |
| q-active-scale-bound | R | = | $4\binom{k}{2}$ | bag size bound k |
| q-cache-capacity | n_0 | = | $2k^2+2$ | bag size bound k |
| q-inclusive-reservations | q_0 | = | $n_0+1$ | bag size bound k |
| q-anchor-upper-factor | U (anchor pseudometric) | = | $3N(N-1)$ | independent of the cache parameters J and eta |
| q-noise-amplitude | c | = | $\alpha/8$ | independent of eta, the launch set and the graph |
| q-noise-probability | rho | = | $\pi/2$ | independent of eta, the launch set and the graph |
| q-final-distortion | C_k (final) | = | $(U_{\mathrm{bag}}+U)/\lambda_k$ | lambda_k comes from a compactness argument without explicit value |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **s-separator** [claim](#stmt-lem-separator) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-separator): Delete a bag node from the tree and use connectedness of vertex occurrence subtrees. -> separation and monotone-distance properties of bags
- **s-gluing** [claim](#stmt-lem-gluing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-gluing): Root the tree, sample each child bag conditionally on its overlap, and prove marginals by induction. -> global cut measure with prescribed bag traces
- **s-markov-flow** [claim](#stmt-thm-markov-flow) / [proof](#stmt-thm-markov-flow): Imported symmetric Markov flow bound from the cited literature. -> flow bounded by constant times endpoint probability
- **s-uncertainty** [claim](#stmt-prop-uncertainty) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-uncertainty): Insert the full testing-bag assignment as a layer, attach a prefix to the suffix measure, and apply the Markov flow bound to the resulting flows. -> separation lower bound from uncertainty
- **s-height-gap** [claim](#stmt-lem-height-gap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-height-gap): A constant assignment on the testing set over a threshold interval gives opposite labels at the target on each half. -> uncertainty at least the gap
- **s-launch-gap** [claim](#stmt-lem-launch-gap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-launch-gap): Thresholds between the two perturbed heights agree on the launch bag and differ at the target. -> uncertainty and separation lower bounds for paired sweeps
- **s-trace-update** [claim](#stmt-lem-trace-update) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-trace-update): Sweep masses on an ordered set are affine in the heights, so averaging preserves the trace. -> trace-preserving update
- **s-particle-bounds** [claim](#stmt-lem-particle-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-particle-bounds): Induction on a quadratic bound for sums over index subsets, preserved by averaging with one unit of perturbation. -> uniform Lipschitz and height bounds
- **s-particle-realization** [claim](#stmt-prop-particle-realization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-particle-realization): Choose the first eligible free pair by weak order, copy memory per child, and record bag laws as traces of the total sweep. -> consistent symmetric bag measures with persistence of reserved pairs
- **s-padded** [claim](#stmt-thm-padded) / [proof](#stmt-thm-padded): Adapt the published padded decomposition for bounded treewidth to the paper's constants. -> random partitions with padding probability
- **s-disjoint-bumps** [claim](#stmt-lem-disjoint-bumps) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-disjoint-bumps): Compare values at points in the same set or in different sets using vanishing outside the support. -> Lipschitz bound for combined bumps
- **s-cobag** [claim](#stmt-prop-cobag) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-cobag): Reserve one pair per active scale, perturb with padded bumps, and lower bound expected separation by integrating a uniform coefficient. -> common-bag pseudometric with two-sided bounds
- **s-ordinary-noise** [claim](#stmt-lem-ordinary-noise) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ordinary-noise): Randomly shifted dyadic distance bands with padded bumps and independent coefficients. -> launch perturbation with distance multiplier and local noise
- **s-cache-defined** [claim](#stmt-lem-cache-defined) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cache-defined): Monotone distances from the separator property bound the protected entries by the number of thresholds. -> well-defined bounded cache
- **s-anchor-tests** [claim](#stmt-lem-anchor-tests) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-anchor-tests): Combine launch gap or height gap with uncertainty transport and average over launch randomness. -> launch test and height test
- **s-limiting-rules** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-limiting-rules) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-limiting-rules): Basic properties of a nonprincipal ultrafilter. -> finite eventual alternatives, positive lower bounds, ordering of times
- **s-youngest-crossing** [claim](#stmt-lem-youngest-crossing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-youngest-crossing): Crossing anchors are simultaneously present at an early time, so the cache bound limits their number; the root is crossing. -> youngest crossing anchor
- **s-old-distant** [claim](#stmt-lem-old-distant-anchor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-old-distant-anchor): Rule out a low launch by the launch test and a small distance by a union bound over the noise coefficient. -> old and distant crossing anchor
- **s-obstruction** [claim](#stmt-lem-anchor-obstruction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-anchor-obstruction): Assume no intermediate vertex, split bag vertices into near and far, and obtain a simultaneous height gap from two independent noise sources. -> intermediate distance in every early bag
- **s-protected-successor** [claim](#stmt-lem-protected-successor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-protected-successor): Bound the successor distance via the launch test, then show one of the two thresholds lies between its distance and that of the old anchor. -> protected immediate successor
- **s-younger-crossing** [claim](#stmt-lem-younger-crossing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-younger-crossing): Follow the immediate successor through early tests; protection prevents its eviction so it reaches a late bag. -> crossing anchor younger than the youngest
- **s-combined-lower** [claim](#stmt-prop-combined-lower) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-younger-crossing-2): Contradiction: a sequence of ratios tending to zero produces a crossing anchor younger than the youngest. -> uniform lower comparison constant
- **s-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-younger-crossing-3): Sum the two cut pseudometrics, expand the averaged cut masses and scale coordinates by the lower constant. -> explicit coordinate embedding into finite-dimensional L1

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-particle-bounds**: Under any finite sequence of pair-averaging updates with a bounded perturbation, starting from zero particles, each particle keeps a Lipschitz bound and a height bound that depend only on the particle count and the update parameters, not on the number of updates. The finite numerical core is that the vector of normalized differences keeps total zero, and every index subset sums to at most its size times the number of indices outside it.
- **inv-trace-update**: Replacing two particles that share a weak increasing order on a bag by the average plus and minus a perturbation vanishing on that bag leaves the combined cut law on the bag unchanged.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-triangle-per-update**: Bound the Lipschitz constant of a particle by adding the perturbation bound once per update, using the triangle inequality at each step. -> The bound grows with the number of updates and becomes useless for deep decompositions; the uniform bound needs the joint invariant on sums of differences over all indices.
- **anti-explicit-constant**: Read an explicit numerical value of the distortion constant as a function of the bag bound off this construction. -> The existence of the constant is proved by a compactness argument, so no closed-form dependence is available and any number so produced is unsupported.
- **anti-dominating-trees**: Obtain the uniform embedding by sampling dominating tree metrics and averaging. -> Some series-parallel metrics force expected tree distortion growing with the number of vertices, so this route cannot give a uniform bound.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `algorithmic`. The main theorem is existential and proved by an ultrafilter compactness argument, so it has no finite procedure. The finite numerical core of the particle-update lemma, the pair-averaging update with a bounded perturbation and its subset-sum invariant, is an explicit finite computation, and Panel 3 checks that part only.

```python
# INPUTS: stdin tokens: N, then triples i j e (1-based indices, e a rational string); updates a_i,a_j -> (a_i+a_j)/2 + e, (a_i+a_j)/2 - e
# PRECONDITIONS: N >= 1; each update needs 1 <= i,j <= N, i != j, |e| <= 1; otherwise the output is INVALID
# OUTPUTS: final vector entries as exact fractions separated by single spaces, or the word INVALID
# TIME: O(N + number of updates) exact rational operations
# SPACE: O(N) exact rationals
# ARITHMETIC: exact rational arithmetic with fractions.Fraction, no floating point
# FAILURE: malformed update (equal indices, index out of range, |e| > 1) prints INVALID and exits normally
import sys
from fractions import Fraction


def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    n = int(data[0])
    a = [Fraction(0)] * n
    ops = [(data[t], data[t + 1], data[t + 2]) for t in range(1, len(data), 3)]
    for x, y, z in ops:
        i = int(x) - 1
        j = int(y) - 1
        e = Fraction(z)
        if i == j or abs(e) > 1 or not (0 <= i < n) or not (0 <= j < n):
            print("INVALID")
            return
        m = (a[i] + a[j]) / 2
        a[i] = m + e
        a[j] = m - e
    print(" ".join(str(v) for v in a))


main()
```

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves an existence statement about a distortion constant that depends only on the bag-size bound. The constant has no explicit estimate in k, so no library or API can be specified from it. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The objects are shortest-path metrics of finite weighted graphs and their embeddings into a real L1 space. No simulation dynamics, rules or real-time behaviour appear in the paper. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result is a theorem about graphs of bounded bag size. It gives no checker, solver or analysis workflow beyond what other cited works supply for sparsest-cut approximation. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The embedding is built through an ultrafilter-based compactness argument. The paper gives no explicit procedure with a stated running time. It only notes that, combined with published algorithms for rational lengths, a fixed-parameter approximation follows. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Tree decompositions, bag measures and anchor caches appear only as proof devices. The paper states no query or update interface, and no space or time guarantees for them. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the paper concerns circuits, memory hierarchies or parallel hardware. The particle and reservation bookkeeping is an analytical device. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper contains no adversary model, hardness assumption or security definition. The randomness used is only auxiliary sampling inside the construction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The embedding is an exact worst-case metric statement, not a learned representation. Particles are real functions on the vertices used to build cut measures, not trainable model state. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result bounds the multicommodity flow-cut gap on graphs with bounded bag size by the distortion constant, and so relates to nonuniform sparsest cut. The constant is non-explicit and the paper gives no solver, so it supports only a theoretical guarantee. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The construction is a centralized global analysis over a whole tree decomposition. It describes no message passing, fault model or consensus. Copying memory per child branch is a proof convention, not a protocol. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Within graph theory the result is a uniform bound for bounded bag size and, through the excluded-planar-minor theorem, for graphs excluding a fixed planar minor. It does not extend to all minor-closed families, and the dominating-tree route is shown to fail. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **lim-bag-size-bound** (hypothesis): The embedding theorem requires a tree decomposition with bags of size at most k for a fixed k. The constant depends on k and is not claimed uniform over all graphs. Blocked use: ['Applying the distortion bound to graph families of unbounded treewidth such as expanders or dense meshes']
- **lim-existence-only-constant** (nonconstructive): The proof yields only the existence of the distortion constant. No explicit dependence on k is given, because the lower comparison constant comes from a compactness argument. Blocked use: ['Reading off a numerical distortion value for a given k', 'Certifying a concrete approximation factor on an instance']
- **lim-dominating-trees** (counterexample_scope): Stochastic embedding into dominating trees cannot give a uniform bound for bounded-treewidth graphs, since some series-parallel metrics need expected tree distortion growing with the number of vertices. Blocked use: ['Obtaining a size-independent embedding by averaging sampled dominating tree metrics']
- **lim-minor-closed-scope** (formal_scope): The consequence for minor-closed families covers only graphs excluding a fixed planar graph as a minor. Gluings along larger complete subgraphs remain open. Blocked use: ['Applying the bound to families excluding a non-planar minor', 'Claiming the full minor-closed conjecture']
- **lim-markov-flow-import** (hypothesis): The transport step depends on an imported symmetric Markov flow bound, valid only with at most w states per layer and involutions swapping the endpoint states. Bag size must stay bounded so the state count is bounded. Blocked use: ['Applying uncertainty transport with unbounded bag size', 'Using the flow bound for non-symmetric chains']
- **lim-reservation-budget** (hypothesis): The particle realization needs the maximum number of simultaneous reserved pairs per memory copy fixed in advance, before any geometric scale parameter is chosen. Blocked use: ['Using schedules whose simultaneous reservations are unbounded']
- **lim-ultrafilter-step** (nonconstructive): The lower comparison argument uses a nonprincipal ultrafilter obtained through Zorn's lemma, so the limiting objects are not explicitly constructed and no procedure follows from it. Blocked use: ['Extracting an algorithm or running time from the compactness argument']
- **lim-algorithmic-rational** (edge_case): The algorithmic consequence is stated only for positive rational edge lengths and a fixed approximation parameter, in fixed-parameter time in k. Blocked use: ['Claiming polynomial time independent of k', 'Applying to irrational real lengths without quantization']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 089 (`lean/docs/089.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Planar Graph Metrics Embed into $L_1$ with Constant Distortion](../../preprints/Planar-Graph-Metrics-Embed-into-L1-with-Constant-Distortion-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [$L_1$ Embeddings of Graphs of Bounded Treewidth](../../preprints/L1-Embeddings-of-Graphs-of-Bounded-Treewidth-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The planar case of the Gupta–Newman–Rabinovich–Sinclair embedding problem asks for a universal distortion bound in $L_1$. The formalized result gives one constant $C$ for every finite connected planar graph with arbitrary positive real edge lengths: its weighted shortest-path metric embeds into real $L_1$ with distortion at most $C$. The constant is independent of the number of vertices and the ratios between edge lengths; singleton graphs are included.
>
> The formalized result gives a uniform $L_1$ embedding bound for each bounded-treewidth class. For every bag-size bound $k\ge2$, there is a constant $C(k)$ such that the weighted shortest-path metric of every nonempty finite connected graph with a tree decomposition of bag size at most $k$ embeds into finite-dimensional real $L_1$ with distortion at most $C(k)$. Edge lengths may be arbitrary positive real numbers, and the constant is independent of the graph and decomposition size.

| declaration | kind |
|---|---|
| `walkLength` | def |
| `shortestPathDistance` | def |
| `HasTreeDecomposition` | def |
| `HasL1Embedding` | def |
| `MainStatement` | def |
| `main_theorem` | theorem |

Comparator source `BoundedTreewidthL1.lean` (1651 bytes, sha256 `630b947fa99b012e1575ad23cf658b0d6e8660d31a95600f5b5894bb0fe5d48f`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section

namespace BoundedTreewidthL1

universe u_1

variable {V : Type u_1} (G : SimpleGraph V)

/-- Sum of the given positive real edge lengths along a graph walk. -/
def walkLength (ℓ : G.edgeSet → ℝ) : {u v : V} → G.Walk u v → ℝ
  | _, _, .nil => 0
  | u, _, .cons (v := v) h p => ℓ ⟨s(u, v), h⟩ + walkLength ℓ p

noncomputable def shortestPathDistance (ℓ : G.edgeSet → ℝ) (u v : V) : ℝ :=
  sInf {r : ℝ | ∃ p : G.Walk u v, p.IsPath ∧ walkLength G ℓ p = r}

def HasTreeDecomposition [DecidableEq V] (k : ℕ) : Prop :=
  ∃ n : ℕ, ∃ T : SimpleGraph (Fin n), ∃ bag : Fin n → Finset V,
    T.IsTree ∧
    (∀ v : V, ∃ t, v ∈ bag t) ∧
    (∀ u v : V, G.Adj u v → ∃ t, u ∈ bag t ∧ v ∈ bag t) ∧
    (∀ v : V, (T.induce {t | v ∈ bag t}).Connected) ∧
    (∀ t, (bag t).card ≤ k)

/-- A map to genuine finite-dimensional real ℓ₁, not just a cut pseudometric. -/
def HasL1Embedding (ℓ : G.edgeSet → ℝ) (C : ℝ) : Prop :=
  ∃ m : ℕ, ∃ F : V → PiLp (1 : ENNReal) (fun _ : Fin m => ℝ),
    ∀ u v : V,
      shortestPathDistance G ℓ u v ≤ ‖F u - F v‖ ∧
      ‖F u - F v‖ ≤ C * shortestPathDistance G ℓ u v

def MainStatement : Prop :=
  ∀ k : ℕ, 2 ≤ k → ∃ C : ℝ, 1 ≤ C ∧
    ∀ (V : Type) [Fintype V] [DecidableEq V] [Nonempty V]
      (G : SimpleGraph V),
      G.Connected → HasTreeDecomposition G k →
      ∀ ℓ : G.edgeSet → ℝ, (∀ e, 0 < ℓ e) → HasL1Embedding G ℓ C

theorem main_theorem : MainStatement := by
  sorry

end BoundedTreewidthL1

end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


For every fixed treewidth bound, the shortest-path metrics of finite connected graphs with arbitrary positive real edge lengths embed into real <i>L</i><sub>1</sub> with uniformly bounded distortion. This resolves the bounded-treewidth case of the Gupta–Newman–Rabinovich–Sinclair conjecture positively.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

A graph metric is the shortest-path metric $d_{G,\ell}$ of a finite connected undirected graph $G=(V,E)$ with positive real edge lengths $\ell:E\to(0,\infty)$. An embedding into real $L_1$ has *distortion at most $C$* if it can be rescaled so that every image distance is between the original distance and $C$ times that distance. We establish a bound depending only on the treewidth.

A *tree decomposition* consists of a finite tree $T$ and subsets $B_t\subseteq V$, called bags, indexed by $t\in V(T)$: the bags cover $V$, each graph edge has both endpoints in a bag, and the bags containing any fixed vertex induce a connected subtree of $T$. Its width is $\max_t|B_t|-1$; the treewidth of $G$ is the minimum width of a tree decomposition. Throughout this paper $k$ bounds *bag cardinality*, so the corresponding treewidth bound is $k-1$.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

<a id="eq:main-embedding"></a>
**Theorem 1**. *For every integer $k\ge2$ there is a finite constant $C_k\ge1$ with the following property. If a finite connected graph $G=(V,E)$ admits a tree decomposition with bags of size at most $k$, then, for every edge-length function $\ell:E\to(0,\infty)$, there is a map $F$ from $V$ into a real $L_1$ space such that $$\label{eq:main-embedding}
 d_{G,\ell}(u,v)
 \le \|F(u)-F(v)\|_1
 \le C_k\,d_{G,\ell}(u,v)
 \qquad(u,v\in V).$$ The constant is independent of the number of vertices, the depth of the decomposition, and all ratios of edge lengths.*

</div>

The target can be taken to be a finite-dimensional real $\ell_1$ space. The proof gives the existence of $C_k$, without an explicit estimate for its dependence on $k$. The bounded-treewidth family is one of the cases of the conjecture of Gupta, Newman, Rabinovich, and Sinclair, which predicts a uniform $L_1$ distortion bound for every proper minor-closed graph family <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[GNRS](#ref-GNRS)]. By the excluded-planar-minor theorem of Robertson and Seymour <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[RS86Planar](#ref-RS86Planar)], our result also gives a uniform bound for graphs excluding any fixed planar graph as a minor.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Context and prior work

<a id="eq:intro-cut-representation"></a>
Finite $L_1$ pseudometrics are exactly the nonnegative combinations of cut pseudometrics. If $S\subseteq V$ and $\delta_S(u,v)=|\mathbf1_S(u)-\mathbf1_S(v)|$, then $$\label{eq:intro-cut-representation}
 D(u,v)=\sum_{S\subseteq V}w_S\delta_S(u,v),\qquad w_S\ge0,$$ is realized by the vectors $(w_S\mathbf1_S(v))_{S\subseteq V}$. Conversely, threshold sweeps through real coordinates give this representation for any finite set of $L_1$ points. We therefore seek cuts that charge graph edges by at most a constant times their lengths and separate every vertex pair by a comparable amount. The edge bound extends along shortest paths to all pairs.

Linial, London, and Rabinovich related low-distortion embeddings to approximate multicommodity flow–cut bounds <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[LLR95](#ref-LLR95)]; Aumann and Rabani independently obtained a general approximate max-flow/min-cut theorem <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[AR98](#ref-AR98)]. Given nonnegative edge capacities and nonnegative demands on unordered pairs of distinct vertices, not all zero, let $\lambda_*$ be the largest common multiplier of the demands that can be routed fractionally, and let $\phi_*$ be the minimum ratio of crossing capacity to separated demand over cuts with positive separated demand. For instances with $\lambda_*>0$, the ratio $\phi_*/\lambda_*$ is the flow–cut gap. Minimizing the cut ratio that defines $\phi_*$ is the nonuniform sparsest-cut problem. The worst such gap on a fixed graph equals the worst $L_1$ distortion of its weighted shortest-path metrics <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[GNRS](#ref-GNRS), Section 3, Theorem 3.2]. For graphs with bags of size at most $k$, Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> therefore gives $$\lambda_*\leq\phi_*\leq C_k\lambda_*.$$

Gupta et al. proved the treewidth-two case <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[GNRS](#ref-GNRS)]. The optimal distortion is $2$, by the upper bound of Chakrabarti, Jaffe, Lee, and Vincent <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[CJLV08](#ref-CJLV08)] and the matching lower bound of Lee and Raghavendra <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[LR10](#ref-LR10)]. Other structural restrictions also yielded uniform bounds: a fixed number of successive outer-face removals in a planar graph <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[CGNRS06](#ref-CGNRS06)], and graphs formed by identifying edges of pieces from a fixed finite family <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[LP13](#ref-LP13)]. A path decomposition is a tree decomposition whose indexing tree is a path; its minimum width is the graph’s *pathwidth*. Lee and Sidiropoulos established the bounded-pathwidth case through stochastic embeddings into trees: each sampled tree metric dominates the graph metric, while its expected expansion is bounded by a function of the pathwidth <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[LeeSidiropoulos](#ref-LeeSidiropoulos)]. Abraham, Filtser, Gupta, and Neiman subsequently obtained $O(\sqrt p)$ distortion into $L_1$ for pathwidth $p$ <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[AFGN22](#ref-AFGN22)]. The route through dominating trees cannot yield a uniform bound for all bounded-treewidth graphs: some series-parallel metrics require expected tree distortion $\Omega(\log n)$ <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[GNRS](#ref-GNRS), Theorem 5.6]. We instead construct cut measures directly on a tree decomposition, allowing arbitrary branching.

A closer precursor is the local-consistency theorem of Chlamtáč, Krauthgamer, and Raghavendra <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[CKR](#ref-CKR), Corollary 1.2]. For treewidth $t$, they obtain bounded $L_1$ distortion when the restrictions of the metric to sets of $t+3$ vertices admit isometric cut representations whose distributions agree on overlaps. Their work supplies both the Markov gluing of consistent local laws and the bounded-state Markov-flow theorem used below. Here we construct the bag laws directly and prove separation without assuming such locally consistent isometric representations of the original metric.

The same line of work developed approximation algorithms for nonuniform sparsest cut using stronger relaxations <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[CKR](#ref-CKR), [GuptaTalwarWitmer](#ref-GuptaTalwarWitmer), [ChalermsookEtAl](#ref-ChalermsookEtAl), [CohenAddadMomkeVerdugo](#ref-CohenAddadMomkeVerdugo)]. These contain consistency information beyond the metric relaxation whose gap is measured by $L_1$ distortion, so their guarantees do not by themselves give the embedding theorem. There is nevertheless an algorithmic consequence of our result. Gupta, Talwar, and Witmer also approximate the minimum $L_1$ distortion <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[GuptaTalwarWitmer](#ref-GuptaTalwarWitmer), Section 3.3.1], and Cohen-Addad, Mömke, and Verdugo obtain fixed-parameter running time for this task <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[CohenAddadMomkeVerdugo](#ref-CohenAddadMomkeVerdugo)]. For positive rational edge lengths, these algorithms and Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> yield embeddings of distortion at most $(2+\varepsilon)C_k$, for each fixed $\varepsilon>0$, in fixed-parameter time with respect to $k$.

The other published input is the padded-decomposition theorem of Filtser et al. <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[FiltserEtAl](#ref-FiltserEtAl)]. At each scale, it supplies a random partition into small-diameter parts with positive probability that a small ball around any specified vertex stays in one part. The padding parameters depend only on the treewidth. The same work gives the size-dependent $L_1$ distortion bound $O(\sqrt{\log(2+t)\log n})$ for $n$-vertex graphs of treewidth $t$, through its Euclidean embedding estimate <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[FiltserEtAl](#ref-FiltserEtAl)]. Such partitions allow localized Lipschitz perturbations. Conroy and Filtser obtained related logarithmic padding bounds for general excluded-minor classes <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[ConroyFiltser25](#ref-ConroyFiltser25)]. The issue addressed here is how to retain useful perturbations without accumulating a loss over the number of scales or the depth of the decomposition.

The companion paper on planar graph metrics <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[OpenAIPlanar](#ref-OpenAIPlanar)] proves a universal distortion bound for that class. It also combines its planar theorem with Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> and the stochastic reduction of Lee and Sidiropoulos <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[LS09Geometry](#ref-LS09Geometry), Theorem 1.7] to obtain uniform $L_1$ distortion and nonuniform fractional flow–cut bounds for each fixed almost-embeddable family. Roughly, these graphs consist of a part embedded in a fixed surface, boundedly many additional pieces whose bounded-width path decompositions follow face-boundary order, and boundedly many extra vertices with arbitrary adjacencies. The reduction uses $1$-sums, or gluings at single vertices, of planar and bounded-treewidth graphs. Extending this conclusion through gluings along larger complete subgraphs remains a further step toward the full conjecture.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The proof: preserving local laws and transporting separation

For a bag $B$, a *bag cut law* assigns nonnegative masses to the binary labelings $\{0,1\}^B$. Two neighboring laws are consistent when their restrictions to the intersection agree. Because the bags form a tree, consistent laws glue into a global law of cuts: after sampling one bag, sample each neighboring bag conditionally on the labels of its intersection. Section <a href="#sec:cuts" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:cuts">2</a> gives this Markov gluing and its exact marginals. Controlling the separation mass of an edge in its containing bag gives the global upper bound. Consistency alone does not supply a lower bound for vertices in distant bags.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
###### Particles preserve bag laws under repeated updates.

A *particle* is a real function on all graph vertices. Sweeping a threshold through its values gives a cut measure. We keep a fixed number of particles and sum their symmetric threshold measures, which give each cut and its complement equal mass. Suppose two particles have the same weak order on the current bag. Replace them by $f+g$ and $f-g$, where $f$ is their average and the perturbation $g$ vanishes on that bag. The combined cut law on the bag is unchanged: successive threshold masses are affine in the ordered heights. This permits different continuations into the children of a decomposition node while preserving consistency.

Every perturbation has a fixed uniform Lipschitz bound. Nevertheless, repeated updates might appear to increase the particles’ Lipschitz constants without bound. Section <a href="#sec:particles" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:particles">3</a> prevents this by a numerical invariant: a quadratic bound on sums of the largest particle differences controls every individual Lipschitz constant. Averaging two entries and adding opposite perturbations preserves this bound, which depends only on the fixed number of particles. Thus every particle remains uniformly Lipschitz after arbitrarily many updates.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
###### Unchanged particles carry uncertainty along bag paths.

A *reservation* freezes two particles, preventing their use in later updates until the reservation is released. Once the maximum number of simultaneous reservations is fixed, a sufficiently large particle list always leaves two unreserved particles with the same chosen increasing order: there are at most $k!$ such orders on a bag. The functions in a reserved pair remain unchanged, so their two cut measures form a common submeasure of the laws on every bag along the reserved segment. At its launch bag $A$, the pair $f\pm g$ agrees because $g|_A=0$. At a remote vertex $z$, the values may separate by $2|g(z)|$.

The relevant quantity is *uncertainty*: after fixing the binary assignment on a testing bag, take the smaller of the two masses corresponding to the possible labels at $z$, and sum over all assignments. The launch pair has uncertainty at least $2|g(z)|$. Section <a href="#sec:cuts" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:cuts">2</a> proves that a common submeasure transports this uncertainty to separation of the endpoints of a bag path. The proof inserts the full testing-bag assignment into a Markov chain and applies the published symmetric Markov-flow theorem. The chain has at most $2^k$ states per layer, so its loss depends only on $k$, independently of the number of bags.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
###### Two reservation schedules.

Section <a href="#sec:perturbations" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:perturbations">4</a> first constructs a cut pseudometric $D_{\mathrm{bag}}$ that separates all pairs in a common bag. For each bag, only a bounded number of dyadic scales match distances between its vertices. Particles perturbed using padded partitions are reserved along the consecutive bags where a scale remains relevant. This gives both a uniform upper bound on every pair and a uniform lower bound for pairs sharing a bag.

Section <a href="#sec:anchors" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:anchors">5</a> constructs a second cut pseudometric $D_{\mathrm{anc}}$. Here a reserved pair is attached to a launch bag, called its *anchor*. A bounded list of anchors is retained on each descending path of the rooted decomposition. The list protects entries immediately after certain drops in the distances from vertices of the current bag to older launch bags. This rule depends only on the metric and decomposition, before any fresh perturbation is sampled. The rule protects only a bounded number of entries, independently of how small the eventual noise parameter must be.

Bounded bag size thus controls the assignment spaces in transport, the particle orders, the active scales, and the protected anchor entries. It also bounds the constants in the padded partitions used to construct the perturbations.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
###### A younger reservation contradicts the youngest one.

Section <a href="#sec:lower" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:lower">6</a> combines the two metrics in a compactness argument. If their sum had no uniform lower bound, the common-bag comparison would allow us to normalize a sequence of poorly separated pairs so that one endpoint stays a fixed distance from the least common ancestor bag. Along the descending path to that endpoint, the distances from the bags to the endpoint fall to zero. Passing to a limit along a nonprincipal ultrafilter, only boundedly many reservations can survive from bags at a definite distance to bags approaching the endpoint. Choose the youngest such reservation.

Its perturbation contains two independent parts: a random multiple of distance to its launch bag, and local noise near the endpoint. Together they force every testing bag still at a definite distance to contain vertices $p,q$, with $p$ nearest the endpoint, whose mutual distance lies between fixed multiples of the endpoint’s distances to the testing bag and to the launch bag. Otherwise the noise produces a positive height gap and hence separation by uncertainty transport. The proof uses this within-bag distance in the list rule to protect a younger successor. That successor also survives toward the endpoint, contradicting the choice of the youngest reservation.

The resulting positive lower constant depends only on $k$. Section <a href="#sec:conclusion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:conclusion">7</a> adds the two cut pseudometrics and expands their finite cut representation as in <a href="#eq:intro-cut-representation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:intro-cut-representation">[eq:intro-cut-representation]</a>, completing the embedding in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.

<a id="sec:cuts"></a>
### Cut measures and transport along a decomposition

The construction will prescribe a measure on the binary assignments of each bag. Our first task is to combine these measures into one global cut metric. Our second is to show that a cut measure present in every bag law along a path can force separation in the glued metric, even when the two vertices do not lie in a common bag.

Fix an integer $k\geq 2$. Let $G=(V,E)$ be a finite connected simple undirected graph with a nonempty vertex set and positive edge lengths $\ell:E\to(0,\infty)$. Its shortest-path metric $d(u,v)$ is the minimum sum of edge lengths along a path from $u$ to $v$, with $d(u,u)=0$. For a nonempty set $A\subseteq V$, put $$d(x,A)=\min_{a\in A}d(x,a),
 \qquad
 \Delta=\operatorname{diam}(V,d).$$ A tree decomposition consists of a finite tree $T$ and bags $B_t\subseteq V$, indexed by $t\in V(T)$, such that the bags cover $V$, every graph edge has both endpoints in a bag, and $$T_v=\{t\in V(T):v\in B_t\}$$ induces a connected subtree for every $v\in V$. The treewidth of $G$ is the minimum of $\max_t|B_t|-1$ over all its tree decompositions. We assume $|B_t|\leq k$; thus the graph has treewidth at most $k-1$. Bags are regarded as indexed occurrences, even when two bags have the same vertex set.

We may assume that all bags are nonempty. Indeed, deleting an empty bag separates the decomposition tree into components whose unions of bags are disjoint and have no graph edges between them. Connectedness of $G$ implies that exactly one of these components contains graph vertices, and all the others can be discarded. Repeating this operation removes all empty bags.

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### Separators and consistent bag laws

<a id="stmt-lem-separator"></a>
<div id="lem:separator" class="lemma">

**Lemma 2** (Separator properties). *The following statements hold for a tree decomposition of a connected graph.*

1.  *If a bag $B$ lies on the tree path between bags $A$ and $C$, every graph path from a vertex of $A$ to a vertex of $C$ meets $B$.*

2.  *For adjacent bags $A,C$, their intersection separates the unions of bags on the two sides of the corresponding tree edge. In particular, adjacent nonempty bags have nonempty intersection.*

<a id="eq:separator-geodesic"></a>
3.  *If $A_0,\ldots,A_r$ occur in this order on a tree path and $x\in A_r$, then $d(x,A_i)$ is nonincreasing in $i$. Moreover, if $B$ lies between $A$ and a bag containing $x$, some $v\in B$ on a shortest path from $A$ to $x$ satisfies $$\label{eq:separator-geodesic}
      d(x,A)-d(v,A)=d(x,v).$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-separator"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-separator</summary>

*Proof.* Delete the node corresponding to $B$ from $T$. The occurrence subtree of each vertex outside $B$ lies in one resulting component. Two adjacent graph vertices outside $B$ lie in the same component, since some bag contains them both. A graph path avoiding $B$ therefore stays in one component. This proves the first assertion; if $B$ is an endpoint bag, that assertion is immediate.

For a tree edge with endpoint bags $A,C$, a graph vertex occurs on both sides precisely when it belongs to $A\cap C$, by connectedness of its occurrence subtree. An edge joining the two sides away from this intersection would have no bag containing both endpoints. This proves the second assertion. If $A\cap C$ were empty, the nonempty bags $A$ and $C$ would lie in different nonempty components of $G$.

For the third assertion, a shortest path from $A_i$ to $x$ meets $A_{i+1}$ by the first assertion, so $d(x,A_{i+1})\leq d(x,A_i)$. Finally, choose a shortest path from $A$ to $x$ and let $v$ be any of its vertices in $B$. Its initial segment has length $d(v,A)$: a shorter path from $A$ to $v$ would shorten the chosen path to $x$. The remaining segment has length $d(x,v)$, giving <a href="#eq:separator-geodesic" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:separator-geodesic">[eq:separator-geodesic]</a>. ◻

</details>

For a finite set $S$, write $\Omega_S=\{0,1\}^S$. A *labeled cut measure* on $S$ is a finite nonnegative measure on $\Omega_S$. For $R\subseteq S$, its *trace* $\nu|_R$ is the pushforward under restriction to $R$. The global complement map is $\omega\mapsto 1-\omega$; write $\nu^c$ for its pushforward and $$\nu^{\mathrm{sym}}=\frac{\nu+\nu^c}{2}$$ for symmetrization. A measure is *symmetric* if $\nu=\nu^c$. The cut pseudometric represented by $\nu$ is <a id="eq:cut-metric"></a>$$\label{eq:cut-metric}
 D_\nu(u,v)=\nu\{\omega:\omega(u)\ne\omega(v)\}.$$ Symmetrization leaves this pseudometric unchanged.

A family $\mu=(\mu_t)_{t\in V(T)}$ of bag measures is *consistent* if all its measures have the same mass $m>0$ and $$\mu_t|_{B_t\cap B_s}=\mu_s|_{B_t\cap B_s}
 \qquad(ts\in E(T)).$$ The common trace on an intersection $S=B_t\cap B_s$ is denoted by $\mu_S$.

Sampling each bag conditionally on its overlap with the preceding bag is the same Markov sampling principle used by Chlamtáč, Krauthgamer, and Raghavendra in their rounding procedure <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[CKR](#ref-CKR), Section 3.2, Lemmas 3.3–3.4]. We record its finite-measure form, including the precise marginals needed below.

<a id="stmt-lem-gluing"></a>
<div id="lem:gluing" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3** (Markov gluing). *Every consistent family of bag measures has a canonical Markov gluing $\rho_\mu$ on $\Omega_V$, of mass $m$, with trace $\mu_t$ on each bag. It is independent of the chosen root and is symmetric when every bag measure is symmetric. Along a path of bags, the successive intersection assignments, with a vertex label from the corresponding endpoint bag at either end, form a Markov chain under $\rho_\mu/m$. The associated pseudometric $$D_\mu(u,v)=\rho_\mu\{\omega:\omega(u)\ne\omega(v)\}$$ is realized in a finite-dimensional real $L_1$ space.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-gluing"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-gluing</summary>

*Proof.* Root $T$ and sample the root assignment with law $\mu_t/m$. At a child bag $B_s$ with parent bag $B_t$, keep the labels on $S=B_t\cap B_s$ and sample the remaining labels according to the conditional law of $\mu_s$ given that assignment on $S$. Previously assigned vertices in $B_s$ are exactly those in $S$: any earlier occurrence of such a vertex is connected to $s$ through $t$. Consequently the sampling defines an assignment on all of $V$. An induction over the rooted tree, using consistency on $S$, proves that each bag has marginal $\mu_s/m$. Multiply the resulting probability measure by $m$ to obtain $\rho_\mu$.

<a id="eq:gluing-product"></a>
For every full assignment $\omega$ for which all separator factors are positive, its mass is $$\label{eq:gluing-product}
 \rho_\mu(\omega)
 =\frac{\displaystyle\prod_{t\in V(T)}\mu_t(\omega|_{B_t})}
        {\displaystyle\prod_{ts\in E(T)}
          \mu_{B_t\cap B_s}(\omega|_{B_t\cap B_s})}.$$ If a separator assignment has zero mass, the full assignment has zero mass as well, by the already established marginals. Formula <a href="#eq:gluing-product" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:gluing-product">[eq:gluing-product]</a> is independent of the root and invariant under complement when the bag measures are symmetric.

Rooting at the first bag of a path and integrating out branches shows that each next intersection is sampled using only the previous intersection assignment. This also permits an endpoint vertex as the first or last layer, by conditioning the endpoint bag law on that vertex or restricting it to that vertex, respectively. Finally, on the finite measure space $(\Omega_V,\rho_\mu)$, the functions $F_v(\omega)=\omega(v)$ satisfy $\|F_u-F_v\|_1=D_\mu(u,v)$. ◻

</details>

Two immediate consequences will be used repeatedly. First, if the separation mass in a bag containing each graph edge $uv$ is at most $U\ell(uv)$, then the bag marginals and the triangle inequality give <a id="eq:edge-extension"></a>$$\label{eq:edge-extension}
 D_\mu(u,v)\leq U d(u,v)\qquad(u,v\in V).$$ Second, when the prepared laws depend measurably on auxiliary randomness and have an integrable common mass, the average of $\rho_\mu$ is again a finite cut measure. Thus averaging the resulting pseudometrics preserves their $L_1$ representation.

<a id="pm-h-11"></a>
#### A transport inequality for a common submeasure

The glued measure need not preserve the contribution of any one function outside a bag. The next result gives a substitute: if the same cut measure remains present in every bag along a path segment, its uncertainty at the terminal vertex forces separation in the glued metric. The published flow theorem supplies the bound independent of the path’s length; we prove the passage from a common submeasure to a feasible flow.

The external input in this subsection is a theorem of Chlamtáč, Krauthgamer, and Raghavendra. A *Markov flow graph* for a finite Markov chain $X_0,\ldots,X_r$, with state sets $L_0,\ldots,L_r$, has arcs only from $L_{i-1}$ to $L_i$, with capacities $$c_i(a,b)=\Pr[X_{i-1}=a,\ X_i=b].$$ We use the symmetric case: each layer has a fixed-point-free involution $\theta_i$, and the joint law of $(\theta_i(X_i))_{i=0}^r$ equals that of $(X_i)_{i=0}^r$.

<a id="stmt-thm-markov-flow"></a>
<div id="thm:markov-flow" class="theorem">

**Theorem 4** (Symmetric Markov flow bound <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[CKR](#ref-CKR), Theorem 4.4]). *For every integer $w\geq2$ there is a finite constant $C(w)\geq1$ with the following property. Suppose a symmetric Markov flow graph has at most $w$ states per layer, first layer $\{s_0,s_1\}$, and last layer $\{t_0,t_1\}$, and its involutions interchange the two states in each endpoint layer. Every capacity-respecting flow from $s_0$ to $t_1$ has value at most $$C(w)\Pr[X_0=s_0,\ X_r=t_1].$$ The constant is independent of the number of layers and of all transition probabilities.*

</div>

Zero-probability states may be removed in complementary pairs: their incident capacities vanish, so neither feasible flows nor endpoint probabilities change. Deterministic transitions are allowed.

<a id="eq:bk-definition"></a>
<a id="eq:uncertainty-symmetrization"></a>
For the rest of the proof fix $$\label{eq:bk-definition}
 b_k=C(2^k)^{-1}>0.$$ For a labeled cut measure $\nu$ on $V$, a set $P\subseteq V$, and $z\in V$, define its *uncertainty at $z$ given $P$* by <a id="eq:uncertainty-definition"></a>$$\label{eq:uncertainty-definition}
 E_\nu(z\mid P)
 =\sum_{b\in\Omega_P}
   \min\bigl\{\nu(\omega|_P=b,\omega(z)=0),
